And it’s started to show there too—my supervisor brought up the missed cycle-count deadlines during a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you didn’t feel any pleasure or enjoyment, even when doing things you used to really like, like basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve noticed that. Even with basketball or sketching, it’s not really enjoyable the way it used to be. I’ve kind of stopped showing up for basketball and I don’t make my usual time to sketch anymore. Work just feels like it’s taking over my life, and it’s even showing there—my supervisor had to bring up missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you completely lost interest in doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching—even when you weren’t feeling physically tired or stressed about work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely lost interest when I wasn’t tired or thinking about work. It’s kind of hard to separate it out. I know I haven’t been doing those things much, but I can’t really say there was a clear time where I just had no interest for no reason.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.22, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work have been getting to me. Since they made me the acting workflow lead, I walk into the warehouse already tense. I’ve been snapping over little SAP errors, missing cycle-count deadlines I normally wouldn’t miss, and by the time I get home I’m too drained to play basketball or even work on my sketches.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying activities like basketball or sketching as much as you used to since these work issues started?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, I used to make time for basketball and sketching pretty regularly, but lately I just… don’t show up for it. Work feels like it’s taking over everything. Even my supervisor had to bring up the missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in, so it’s not just in my head. By the time I’m done, I don’t have much left for the stuff I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even the thought of playing basketball or sketching didn’t seem appealing or worth the effort?\n\nPatient: Yeah, recently even thinking about going to basketball or sitting down to sketch feels like too much effort. I just don’t show up for it anymore. Work has been taking over my life, and it’s affecting things there too—my supervisor had to bring up the missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even the thought of doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, didn’t seem appealing at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s happened. I’ll think about going to play basketball or sitting down to sketch, and it just feels like more than I can deal with. I’ve basically stopped showing up for those things because work feels like it’s taking over. And it’s started to show there too—my supervisor brought up the missed cycle-count deadlines during a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you didn’t feel any pleasure or enjoyment, even when doing things you used to really like, like basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve noticed that. Even with basketball or sketching, it’s not really enjoyable the way it used to be. I’ve kind of stopped showing up for basketball and I don’t make my usual time to sketch anymore. Work just feels like it’s taking over my life, and it’s even showing there—my supervisor had to bring up missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you completely lost interest in doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching—even when you weren’t feeling physically tired or stressed about work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely lost interest when I wasn’t tired or thinking about work. It’s kind of hard to separate it out. I know I haven’t been doing those things much, but I can’t really say there was a clear time where I just had no interest for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you also lost interest in other things outside of work and your usual hobbies, like seeing friends, watching your favorite shows, or anything else you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way with other stuff. Mostly it’s been the basketball and sketching, and I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to things like shows or seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from activities you used to enjoy, even things like spending time with friends or watching something you like on TV?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I get no pleasure at all. I haven’t really been paying close attention with things like TV or seeing friends. Mostly what I notice is I’m just not doing basketball or sketching much lately, but I can’t say everything feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of interest or pleasure in things other than work and your usual hobbies, like spending time with family, seeing friends, or doing other everyday activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t noticed that clearly. I can’t really say I’ve lost interest in family or friends or everyday stuff across the board. It’s more that I haven’t been tracking it that closely, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of interest or pleasure in almost all activities, even ones you used to enjoy a lot, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s almost all activities. I’m not sure I’ve noticed it that broadly. The main things I can point to are basketball and sketching, but I can’t really say everything I used to enjoy feels gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, even when there’s nothing specific going on at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d say that. I do feel down sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day or happening when there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or unmotivated even when things at work were going smoothly or there was no obvious stressor?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really remember having clear days like that where everything was fine and I still felt down for no reason. Usually if I feel off, I tend to connect it to something, but I haven’t tracked it closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in other activities that you used to enjoy, even things like hanging out with friends or doing small things that used to make your day better?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed that, no. I mean, maybe I’m less up for some things here and there, but I can’t say I’ve lost interest in hanging out with friends or all the little stuff that usually helps. It’s more that I don’t always feel as motivated, but not like everything stopped being enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt down or hopeless even when nothing particularly stressful or upsetting was happening at work or in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I haven’t noticed it happening out of nowhere when everything else is okay.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Work just feels like it’s taking over my life, and it’s even showing there—my supervisor had to bring up missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you completely lost interest in doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching—even when you weren’t feeling physically tired or stressed about work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely lost interest when I wasn’t tired or thinking about work. It’s kind of hard to separate it out. I know I haven’t been doing those things much, but I can’t really say there was a clear time where I just had no interest for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you also lost interest in other things outside of work and your usual hobbies, like seeing friends, watching your favorite shows, or anything else you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way with other stuff. Mostly it’s been the basketball and sketching, and I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to things like shows or seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from activities you used to enjoy, even things like spending time with friends or watching something you like on TV?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I get no pleasure at all. I haven’t really been paying close attention with things like TV or seeing friends. Mostly what I notice is I’m just not doing basketball or sketching much lately, but I can’t say everything feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of interest or pleasure in things other than work and your usual hobbies, like spending time with family, seeing friends, or doing other everyday activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t noticed that clearly. I can’t really say I’ve lost interest in family or friends or everyday stuff across the board. It’s more that I haven’t been tracking it that closely, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of interest or pleasure in almost all activities, even ones you used to enjoy a lot, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s almost all activities. I’m not sure I’ve noticed it that broadly. The main things I can point to are basketball and sketching, but I can’t really say everything I used to enjoy feels gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, even when there’s nothing specific going on at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d say that. I do feel down sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day or happening when there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or unmotivated even when things at work were going smoothly or there was no obvious stressor?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really remember having clear days like that where everything was fine and I still felt down for no reason. Usually if I feel off, I tend to connect it to something, but I haven’t tracked it closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in other activities that you used to enjoy, even things like hanging out with friends or doing small things that used to make your day better?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed that, no. I mean, maybe I’m less up for some things here and there, but I can’t say I’ve lost interest in hanging out with friends or all the little stuff that usually helps. It’s more that I don’t always feel as motivated, but not like everything stopped being enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt down or hopeless even when nothing particularly stressful or upsetting was happening at work or in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. When I’ve felt down, I can usually point to something that was bothering me. I haven’t noticed it happening out of nowhere when everything else is okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt like nothing you did was enjoyable, even things you used to really like, not just work or your usual hobbies?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a stretch where nothing felt enjoyable at all. There are times I’m not really in the mood or I don’t feel like putting in the effort, but I wouldn’t say the things I usually like stopped feeling good completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting most of the day, nearly every day, where you’ve lost interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, but not most of the day nearly every day where I couldn’t enjoy anything. Work has been taking over my life, and it’s affecting things there too—my supervisor had to bring up the missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when even the thought of doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching, didn’t seem appealing at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s happened. I’ll think about going to play basketball or sitting down to sketch, and it just feels like more than I can deal with. I’ve basically stopped showing up for those things because work feels like it’s taking over. And it’s started to show there too—my supervisor brought up the missed cycle-count deadlines during a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you didn’t feel any pleasure or enjoyment, even when doing things you used to really like, like basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve noticed that. Even with basketball or sketching, it’s not really enjoyable the way it used to be. I’ve kind of stopped showing up for basketball and I don’t make my usual time to sketch anymore. Work just feels like it’s taking over my life, and it’s even showing there—my supervisor had to bring up missed cycle-count deadlines in a performance check-in.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you completely lost interest in doing things you used to enjoy, like basketball or sketching—even when you weren’t feeling physically tired or stressed about work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely lost interest when I wasn’t tired or thinking about work. It’s kind of hard to separate it out. I know I haven’t been doing those things much, but I can’t really say there was a clear time where I just had no interest for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you also lost interest in other things outside of work and your usual hobbies, like seeing friends, watching your favorite shows, or anything else you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way with other stuff. Mostly it’s been the basketball and sketching, and I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to things like shows or seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from activities you used to enjoy, even things like spending time with friends or watching something you like on TV?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I get no pleasure at all. I haven’t really been paying close attention with things like TV or seeing friends. Mostly what I notice is I’m just not doing basketball or sketching much lately, but I can’t say everything feels completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a lack of interest or pleasure in things other than work and your usual hobbies, like spending time with family, seeing friends, or doing other everyday activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t noticed that clearly. I can’t really say I’ve lost interest in family or friends or everyday stuff across the board. It’s more that I haven’t been tracking it that closely, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant loss of interest or pleasure in almost all activities, even ones you used to enjoy a lot, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s almost all activities. I’m not sure I’ve noticed it that broadly. The main things I can point to are basketball and sketching, but I can’t really say everything I used to enjoy feels gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, even when there’s nothing specific going on at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d say that. I do feel down sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day or happening when there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or unmotivated even when things at work were going smoothly or there was no obvious stressor?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really remember having clear days like that where everything was fine and I still felt down for no reason. Usually if I feel off, I tend to connect it to something, but I haven’t tracked it closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in other activities that you used to enjoy, even things like hanging out with friends or doing small things that used to make your day better?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed that, no. I mean, maybe I’m less up for some things here and there, but I can’t say I’ve lost interest in hanging out with friends or all the little stuff that usually helps. It’s more that I don’t always feel as motivated, but not like everything stopped being enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt down or hopeless even when nothing particularly stressful or upsetting was happening at work or in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. When I’ve felt down, I can usually point to something that was bothering me. I haven’t noticed it happening out of nowhere when everything else is okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt like nothing you did was enjoyable, even things you used to really like, not just work or your usual hobbies?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a stretch where nothing felt enjoyable at all. There are times I’m not really in the mood or I don’t feel like putting in the effort, but I wouldn’t say the things I usually like stopped feeling good completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting most of the day, nearly every day, where you’ve lost interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, not just basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’ve had times where I didn’t feel like doing much, but not most of the day nearly every day where I couldn’t enjoy anything. It hasn’t been like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve stopped enjoying or lost interest in activities that you used to care about or look forward to, even things that had nothing to do with work or your usual hobbies like basketball or sketching?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have days where I don’t feel like doing much, but I haven’t noticed that I’ve stopped caring about things across the board or anything like that. It’s more just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy, even when work wasn’t particularly stressful or on your mind?\n\nPatient: I can’t really think of days like that, no. Usually if I’m not enjoying something, it’s because I’m tired or stressed, but I haven’t noticed it happening when work wasn’t on my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
